\section{Conditional probability-stability diagnostics}\label{app:stability}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} Randomized allocation admits a conditional
sensitivity bound, but small field margins and scores near zero limit its
applicability. Stability alone does not imply lower strategic inflation.

Fix the positive-score support $I$, the queried field of each item, and budget
$B$. For two score vectors $g,g'$ with all supported scores at least $m>0$, let
$\delta=\|g-g'\|_\infty$. Define the score-minimax value and canonical marginals
\[
 T(g)=\min_{0\leq p\leq1,\,\sum_i p_i\leq B}\max_{i\in I}g_i(1-p_i),
 \qquad p_i(g)=[1-T(g)/g_i]_+.
\]
For $B=0$ use zero marginals; for $B\geq |I|$ use unit marginals on $I$.
Then
\[
 |T(g)-T(g')|\leq\delta,\qquad
 \|p(g)-p(g')\|_\infty\leq\min\{1,2\delta/m\}.
\]
For any fixed feasible allocation, the two objectives differ by at most
$\delta$, which proves the value bound after minimization. The function
$h(t,x)=[1-t/x]_+$ is separately $1/m$-Lipschitz in $t$ and $x$ for
$t\geq0,x\geq m$: on its positive branch, $t/x^2\leq1/x$, and it is
continuous at the branch boundary. Applying the value bound proves the
marginal bound. The active allocation set may change; positive-score support
and queried fields must remain fixed.

If both pre-query and recovered quotes change by at most $\epsilon$, and the
primed recovery span is at most $R$, expected quotes differ by at most
$\epsilon+R\min\{1,2\delta/m\}$. If these conditions hold uniformly over
all own reports at fixed peer reports, costs are unchanged, and truthful
quotes are verification-neutral, the positive unilateral-gain envelope changes
by at most $2\epsilon+R\min\{1,2\delta/m\}$. This is not a guarantee for
adapted peer responses, equilibrium selection, training convergence, or a
fixed realization of the sampling randomness.

Three examples delimit the claim. With two items and $B=1$, swapping scores
$(1+\delta,1)$ and $(1,1+\delta)$ changes the first item's top-$B$ probability
by one, but its minimax probability by $\delta/(2+\delta)$. In contrast,
swapping $(2\delta,\delta)$ and $(\delta,2\delta)$ changes its minimax
probability by $1/3$ even as $\delta$ tends to zero. Finally, one fully queried
item with two fields whose scores swap as in the first example can have a
unit quote jump if the two recovery quotes are zero and one. The score lower
bound and fixed-field condition therefore cannot simply be removed.
An exact-arithmetic checker covers 2,916 score-pair/budget cases, including
510 active-set changes; 108 production/linear-program comparisons agree to
floating-point precision. These finite checks supplement the proof.

We additionally audited all 1,080 saved fixed-profile batch executions at
hypothetical radii $\delta=\alpha\operatorname{median}(g_i:g_i>0)$.
A sufficient certificate requires support preservation and every positive
item's top-two field gap to exceed $2\delta$. Its bound uses
$m=\min_{i\in I}g_i-\delta$. Recovery spans are reconstructed for every
eligible item, including items not queried by a saved top-$B$ allocation.
Table~\ref{tab:stability} records the sufficient-certificate counts.
\begin{table}[htbp]\centering
\begin{tabular}{rrrr}
\toprule
$\alpha$ & Minimax conditions & Probability bound $<1$ & Top-$B$ constant\\
\midrule
0.001 & 471 & 468 & 401\\
0.01 & 401 & 353 & 212\\
0.05 & 267 & 245 & 53\\
0.1 & 244 & 242 & 39\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Post-hoc stability certificates at four hypothetical relative score radii. Each row covers the same 1,080 dependent fixed-profile batch executions. Counts certify sufficient conditions, rather than observed perturbation errors or a ranking of performance.}\label{tab:stability}\end{table}
Each row has 1,080 batch executions, including both generating allocators;
these are dependent diagnostic cases, not independent statistical replicates.
Top-$B$ is locally constant when the same support/field conditions and its
selection-rank margin hold (or all positive items fit). Its certificate is
stronger than a nonzero sensitivity bound, so counts do not establish an
ordering of stability. Failure to certify is not evidence of instability.
A separate checker reconstructs all 4,320 condition rows and 144 grouped
summaries using exact fractions of the stored scores and radii; displayed
quote bounds remain floating-point approximations. The radii are not calibrated
prediction errors. These post-hoc diagnostics neither establish performance
improvement nor explain source effects causally. General water-filling
sensitivity is established literature; priority for this application-specific
bound is not claimed.

\section{Strong additional comparators and adaptation boundaries}\label{app:comparators}
The following controls belong to earlier separately corroborated experiments,
not the newly trained matched factorial model family. Keeping these identities
separate prevents a favorable acquisition or tuning result from being attributed
to the wrong training intervention.

\subsection{Ordinary-benefit and equal-allowance controls}
A robust/no-feedback price model with ordinary error-reduction acquisition was
evaluated on all six budgets and the original three seeds. The independently
checked 54-cell extension retained identical honest quotes, public scoring,
allocations and first-batch exact deviations. At 10\% capacity, reference
inflation was 0.01077, 0.01117 and 0.00778 on synthetic, JUCars and MUCars,
respectively. Reference-minus-SAV differences were $-0.00998$, $-0.00432$
and $0.00089$, with conditional shared-batch intervals
$[-0.01260,-0.00791]$, $[-0.00598,-0.00285]$ and $[0.00045,0.00130]$.
These intervals averaged the three fitted seeds before resampling batches;
they are not the ten-seed crossed intervals in the main factorial analysis.

An additional size/tuning study retained training caps 1,000, 2,500 and 5,000,
three fitting seeds, three training-internal gap-weight trials and twelve
residual epochs for both candidates. Its 972 parent tuning cells and 324
ordinary-benefit reference cells passed full audits. At the largest cap,
tuned SAV inflation was 20.41\% lower on MUCars but 30.09\% higher on
synthetic and 27.09\% higher on JUCars than the separately tuned ordinary-benefit
reference. The associated reference-minus-SAV intervals were
$[0.00097,0.00186]$, $[-0.00682,-0.00204]$ and $[-0.00333,-0.00125]$.
All scheduled caps and fixed/tuned roles remain in the archived evidence;
no favorable training size replaces the largest-cap comparison. This further
supports conditional gains rather than a consistent candidate advantage.

\subsection{Categorical support-margin imputation}
A whole-field adaptation of support-margin imputation (SMI)~\cite{smi} used
all allowed covariates and four optional-field groups. One deterministic
fitting generation per source trained a sixteen-model linear support-vector
regression family on complete-optional-field training rows. Scaling, category
vocabularies and target normalization were training-only. Missing categories
required a one-hot completion including an unknown token. If the reduced
sign-consistent model family or its optimization was unavailable, the method
used ordinary median/mode imputation. The original SMI guarantee is not
asserted on these fallback rows, and this is not an unchanged official reproduction.

An independent categorical-enumeration and scalar-interval auditor checked
94,527 report/domain cases and every final quotation and legal no-query report.
Training cohort sizes were 2,947, 2,674 and 3,368 for synthetic, JUCars and
MUCars. Across unrestricted, nonnegative and unit-box completion domains,
aggregate outcomes matched ordinary median/mode regression. Attacked fallback
fractions were 75.3\%, 87.3\% and 65.6\%, respectively. This diagnoses a limit
of the categorical adaptation's retained-family assumptions; it does not
refute the original SMI theorem. These no-query fits differ in cohort and
model class from the matched factorial predictor, so their higher inflation
cannot isolate a benefit of the present acquisition procedure.

\subsection{Acquisition-conditioned neighbor control}
ACO-1 adapted acquisition-conditioned expected-loss scoring~\cite{activeoracle}
to one-field regression queries and batch capacity. A training-only neighbor
bank estimated loss changes for sixteen observation patterns; unavailable
recorded bank values gave zero replay benefit. Distances used observed query
groups and permitted nonoptional features, with fixed category/numeric weights
and stable ties. It was a clean-room task adaptation, not an unchanged execution
of the original sequential classification method.

The separately corroborated nine-pair study contained 144 cells. The declared
main $k=25$ used every capacity; fixed $k=10$ and $k=50$ sensitivities used
10\% capacity only. No winning $k$ was selected. With the frozen robust
predictor and $k=25$, mean inflation was 0.01081 on synthetic, 0.01118 on
JUCars and 0.00716 on MUCars. Full-SAV-minus-ACO differences of 0.00995 and
0.00431 on synthetic and JUCars had conditional intervals
$[0.00725,0.01345]$ and $[0.00291,0.00588]$. On MUCars the point comparison
favored SAV by 0.00027, but the interval crossed zero. Thus a strong predictive
acquisition control can outperform the original candidate. These results
strengthen comparator coverage without implying that one-step ACO-1 dominates
the matched factorial policies or reproduces the original method's guarantee.

\section{Conditional uncertainty and mechanism diagnostics}\label{app:mechanism_diagnostics}
\subsection{Exact conditional crossed-bootstrap variance}
Let $Y$ be a paired-difference matrix with $S$ fitting seeds and $K$ batches.
Independent bootstrap seed weights $U$ and record-normalized batch weights
$V$ give $U^\top YV$. Write $m=\mathbb E[V]$, $C=\operatorname{Cov}(V)$,
$r=Ym-\mathbf1\,\operatorname{mean}(Ym)$,
$z=\operatorname{mean}_{s}(Y)$ and
$R=Y-\mathbf1z^\top-r\mathbf1^\top$. The conditional variance decomposes as
\[
 \frac{\|r\|^2}{S^2}+z^\top Cz+
 \frac{1}{S^2}\sum_s R_s^\top C R_s.
\]
These are seed-main, batch-main and interaction terms. Batch-weight moments
are calculated by conditioning on the multinomial count of the single
partial batch, retaining ratio normalization. Exhaustive balanced and
unequally weighted $2\times2$ resampling independently checks the formula.
All 48 capped/full real-source feedback decompositions are retained.
Capped components use ten equal 100-record batches; uncapped MUCars components
use 107 batches normalized by record counts, including a 72-record tail.
Thus the outcome matrix and averaging weights both change. For top-$B$ and
minimax, batch variances fall by factors 5.73 and 8.04 (not 10.7), while
seed-main variances change by $-13.20\%$ and $-9.43\%$. This is not variance
reduction from sample size alone. Holding all 107 batches fixed and changing
only equal-batch to record-count weights changes the seed term by merely
$-0.16\%$ and $+0.11\%$, respectively; the larger change cannot be attributed
to tail weighting alone.

Thirty-seed projections divide seed and interaction terms by three; the
batch-noise-free projection holds the empirical batch distribution fixed.
Neither supplies new training runs, equivalence evidence or population coverage.

\subsection{Concealment identification from saved utility summaries}
For one listing let $C_i=c_i k_i$ be concealment cost, $G_i$ positive expected
quote inflation, $N_i$ the positive best-legal-utility gain over truth, and
$\rho_i$ remaining unilateral regret. Since truthful utility is its honest
quote,
\[
 C_i-\rho_i\le G_i-N_i\le C_i.
\]
Thus $\max(\overline G-\overline N,0)$ bounds mean cost below; adding
$\max_i\rho_i$ and capping by $2\overline c$ bounds it above. Uniform-cost
hidden-field bounds divide by $c$; heterogeneous costs in $[.01,.03)$ give
bounds using .03 and .01, respectively. Dividing by mean heterogeneous cost
would not identify mean hidden fields. Positive-cost states were not saved;
all 20 nonconverged batches remain in the bounds. The inequality passes
768 exhaustive toy utility/returned-state checks. The paired zero-cost
concealment counts use saved states directly. Neither co-occurrence nor this
accounting identity identifies a causal mediation effect.

\subsection{Restricted final-record honest-error pilot}
Three frozen robust/no-feedback models per real source (seeds 13, 42 and
2026) enumerate completions of transmission, fuel and body condition from
their training-encoder vocabularies, including missing tokens. Mileage must
be observed and fixed; other covariates stay fixed. Unsupported observed
categories and missing mileage abstain. The same original 1,000 records per
source are used without tuning or retraining. Current records are included
in the minima; singleton sets preserve their original quotes exactly.
Shared-support errors and abstentions are reported separately.

\caption{The restricted final-record envelope's honest-error cost is concentrated on naturally incomplete categorical records. (a) Among each source's original 1,000 listings, complete/incomplete/abstained counts are 800/1/199 for JUCars and 971/28/1 for MUCars. Complete means all three declared categorical fields are observed; missing mileage causes abstention. Complete covered records have singleton completion sets and exactly zero change. (b) Every MUCars fitting seed is shown for all 999 covered records and its nested 28-record naturally incomplete subset; lines link the two descriptive means for the same frozen model, without interpolation or confidence intervals. Positive honest-MAE change is worse; units are MUCars currency. Mileage remains observed and fixed. This does not validate the full four-field game or unseen-category coverage.}
\label{fig:envelope_boundary}
\end{figure}

Each JUCars model evaluates 806 completions; each MUCars model evaluates
1,139. All baseline quotes match saved predictions within $5\times10^{-6}$
in log quote; sampled completions also match the production all-state
inference route within that tolerance. Predictor primitives are shared.
On the declared covered domain, the envelope is exactly costless for complete
categorical records: 800/801 JUCars and 971/999 MUCars records have singleton
completion sets. All quotation and error changes are confined to the one and
28 naturally incomplete records, respectively. For the MUCars subset, the
largest individual quotation reduction is 25.5\% of that record's original
predicted quote, while mean honest-MAE increases range from 374.24 to 851.51
currency units (Table~\ref{tab:final_record_pilot}). Quotation discounts and
absolute-error costs have different denominators and must not be conflated.
The practical trade-off is zero change on complete covered categorical
records but potentially substantial discounts on naturally incomplete ones.
The guarantee covers only
the declared categorical domain with fixed observed mileage. It does not
cover mileage concealment, unseen values or the original full four-field
game, and this pilot supplies no confirmatory or universal method advantage.


\clearpage
\section{Controlled completion protocols and full comparison evidence}\label{app:completion_methods}
\subsection{Recent-method identity and training boundaries}
The adapters use the same fixed price backends as ACO-1, not each paper's
original predictive task. SEFA, GDFS, DIME and explanation-ranked AFA learn
acquisition using four training-quantile price classes; their scores feed a
shared hard-capacity, one-query allocator. This does not make them native
strategic regressors. Nonquery context is available, natural absence is
unrecoverable, and deployment scores do not observe a hidden value or its
missingness cause. Hidden-mileage perturbation checks left the corresponding
public-state scores unchanged. Predictive information scores and policy
probabilities are used as cross-listing priority proxies without a theorem of
cross-listing cardinal comparability.

SEFA used its official MIT implementation, commit
\texttt{3af8da2}, configurations 1/5/9,
120 epochs per trial and their original latent-sampling settings. GDFS and DIME
used the comparison implementations in that repository, retaining their
relaxed-one-hot/temperature and entropy-scaled value/epsilon-greedy cores.
Their configurations 1/5/9 use 80 predictor-pretraining epochs and 15 epochs
per annealing stage. The task adapter starts from legal public reports and
trains at most two recoveries, uses Adam and stagewise best validation states,
and does not reproduce the original learning-rate scheduler. This is a sourced
task adaptation, not an unchanged original-author reproduction.

Explanation-ranked AFA used the official repository commit
\texttt{d08f39a}, its tabular MLP and
three-layer, four-head GPT, hidden width 128 and embedding width 64.
A fitting-partition 250-tree CatBoost classifier supplies TreeSHAP rankings;
field-value and missing-indicator absolute contributions for the training label
are summed. Natural missingness and numeric category coding are task
adaptations. The stages comprise 250 masked-predictor, 200 ranking-supervised
and 16 policy-trajectory epochs, with at most two recoveries per training
trajectory and a fixed initial action marker at history-free deployment.
Learning rates $0.001,0.0005,0.0003$ form a fixed local grid; the last is an
additional sensitivity value rather than an original default.

All four classification adapters select among three configurations using the
same training-only 80:20 split and then refit on all training records. SEFA
uses its masked validation NLL and selected epoch, while the other three use
mean pre/post-query NLL and their full fixed schedule. Thus selection effort
is documented but their validation objectives are not identical. No evaluation
outcome is used to select a configuration. ACO-1 instead retains the declared
training-bank rule and fixed neighbor sensitivities described in
Appendix~\ref{app:comparators}. These differences, the fixed 1,000-record
completion cohort, and three fitting seeds bound cross-family conclusions.

\subsection{Full component removals and parameter choice}
$S$ switches suppression rather than ordinary-benefit score training, $F$
switches feedback, and $G$ switches the gap penalty. All $2^3$ combinations
share the role separation, epoch budget and reset schedule. The conventional
backbone, mask augmentation, prediction/attack-error and anchor terms remain
when all three switches are removed. Figure~\ref{fig:completion_ablation}
therefore is a complete component ablation, not a comparison with an untrained
or native predictor.

\caption{Complete three-component ablation at 10\% capacity. Circles retain three paired seed differences from S1F1G1 and diamonds show their means. Negative differences indicate lower inflation than the full switched configuration. S/F/G denote suppression-score, feedback and gap switches. Neither number of cells nor number of components is an independent sample size or an innovation count. All nonconverged responses remain in the plot.}
\label{fig:completion_ablation}
\end{figure}

Core sensitivity varied gap weight over $0,0.1,0.25,0.5,1,2$, tolerance over
$0,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1$, and one/two/four reset-feedback rounds at fixed total
12 residual epochs. The design values 0.5 and 0.02 are fixed engineering
choices on the log-quotation scale, not optimal values estimated from these
curves. Increases in gap weight reduce inflation means in this grid, but
inflation alone cannot select a default. Figure~\ref{fig:completion_parameters}
retains both evaluated capacities. A further 144 cells vary anchor weight,
attack-loss weight, learning rate, epochs, action-head tree count and hidden
width one at a time (Figure~\ref{fig:completion_stability}). The defaults are
inserted from the exactly matched S1F1G1 branch, not retrained or interpolated.
All error, failure and convergence metrics remain in the source tables.

\begin{figure}[tbp]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/ten-seed-v1/parameter_defaults.pdf}
\caption{Core-parameter scans in the original feedback implementation, with three fitting seeds. Dotted lines mark design defaults and all evaluated settings are ticked. Gap weight and tolerance use symmetric-log coordinates. Means and full ranges are descriptive, not confidence intervals. The defaults are engineering choices, not evaluation-selected optima.}
\label{fig:completion_parameters}
\end{figure}









\begin{figure}[tbp]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/ten-seed-v1/implementation_defaults.pdf}
\caption{Six implementation-parameter scans at 10\% capacity, retaining three fitting seeds. Every ratio uses the same-seed matched default. Dotted lines mark defaults; ticks show all evaluated values, and learning rate uses a logarithmic axis. Curves show means and full seed ranges, not confidence intervals.}
\label{fig:completion_stability}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Availability-shift stress and source-data coverage}
The availability stress study held fitted models fixed and additionally removed
0, 10\% or 30\% of originally observed query fields using nested stable hashes
of listing and field. Targets were unchanged; newly missing values could not
be recovered. Seven mechanisms (four search/refresh variants, matched
no-feedback, risk and benefit) yielded 189 cells: 63 reused and 126 new
evaluations, with no refitting. Reports were reoptimized after the shift.
This is a controlled availability perturbation, not a new market, MNAR model
or actual-seller validation. Paired metrics, recovery paths and all three
seed values remain in the evidence files. RPL additionally retained low/middle/high
price groups using training-only 10th/90th quantiles and naturally
complete/missing groups; these shared-population subgroups are descriptive.

winners. Currency MAE is not pooled across sources. Identical honest MAE within
a frozen backend is expected and does not demonstrate equal attacked error.

\section{Ten-seed recovery-path diagnostics}\label{app:rpl_extended}
Figure~\ref{fig:rpl_seeds} retains every fitting seed and all four nonzero
path/gap comparisons at primary capacity. Small weights can reverse individual
seed effects; their means do not imply per-seed monotonic improvement. All
zero-weight controls remain in the public source tables, although their
self-difference is identically zero and is not colored as new evidence.

The original three-seed recovery-edge measurement, separate from the ten-seed
performance expansion, gave mean positive gain increments of
0.001790 to 0.000693 (synthetic), 0.001648 to 0.001048 (JUCars), and
0.000788 to 0.000450 (MUCars) at $\lambda=1,\beta=0.5$.
They remained positive, so the zero-edge-penalty premise of
Proposition~\ref{prop:rpl} was not achieved. The pilot is not a ten-seed
mechanism remeasurement or proof of a unique causal explanation.

The new four-stage ACO audit initially stopped because its independent
distance reconstruction subtracted archived float32 bank/query arrays,
whereas production promoted both to float64 before distance calculations.
The corrected, separately versioned auditor matches that arithmetic contract
and passed the full 144-cell chain. The failed receipt, original checker and
new source closure remain public; no tolerance, production model or saved
result was changed to obtain a pass.

\begin{figure}[tbp]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/ten-seed-v1/seed_consistency.pdf}
\caption{All ten fitting seeds at 10\% capacity. Cell values are inflation reductions against each configuration's same-gap zero-path control. Positive values favor RPL; negative values retain adverse seeds. Display values are rounded, retaining one decimal below 1\% magnitude; unrounded data are public. No nonconverged outcome is excluded.}
\label{fig:rpl_seeds}
\end{figure}

}
\caption{Under the original weak training-response search, policy feedback increases mean inflation in all twelve suppression-acquisition cells at the primary 10\% capacity. Points are no-feedback minus policy-feedback paired differences; bars are 95\% unadjusted intervals from 1,000 crossed resamples of ten fitting seeds and ten shared batches. Positive differences would favor feedback. All models use the same fixed training records; 1,000 evaluation listings are reused across seeds. The two MUCars mask-objective intervals cross zero. Other scores are retained in the full contrast data.}
\label{fig:feedback}
\end{figure}

}
\caption{Initial-objective effects depend on the source. With suppression acquisition and no policy feedback, points are mask-objective minus robust-objective inflation differences at 10\% capacity. Positive values favor robust initialization. Intervals and repeated-unit definitions match Fig.~\ref{fig:feedback}. The source labels and row positions encode cohort identity independently of color. These newly trained objectives are not aliases for the original SAV/static-robust candidates.}
\label{fig:initial_objective}
\end{figure}

\section{Additional historical control analyses}\label{app:additional_controls}
The following analyses retain the full original protocols and numerical evidence summarized in the main text. Their original fitting-seed and uncertainty scopes are unchanged.

\subsection{Matched factorial effects}\label{app:factorial_details}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} Under the original weak training-response search,
all 36 primary feedback contrasts increase mean inflation. This documents a
failure of that implementation, not a general negative effect of feedback.

\paragraph{Which conclusion is primary?}
The paper's empirical conclusion is that verification and feedback outcomes
depend on the learning and response procedures. The 36/36 count, Table~\ref{tab:factorial_counts}, and the controls in
Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} establish the
well-audited original implementation's failure pattern. They are not the
headline claim that feedback is intrinsically harmful. The later, four-stage-checked
search intervention (Section~\ref{sec:completion_mechanisms}) largely removes
the synthetic/JUCars mean damage with stronger training search, while MUCars
remains adverse in those means. Both findings must be read together; neither
identifies weak search as the sole cause nor proves exact-equilibrium benefit.
Table~\ref{tab:audit_levels} separates completed computational checks from independent scientific confirmation.

\paragraph{Inference scope.}
Every source uses ten fitting seeds and ten shared evaluation batches of
100 listings. The same 1,000 listings are reused across seeds and methods;
4.32 million outcome records are therefore not independent listing observations.
We report 1,000 crossed seed--batch bootstrap draws and their 2.5th and 97.5th
percentiles. These unadjusted exploratory intervals condition on the fixed
training records. They do not cover a new training population or correct for
multiple comparisons. The primary capacity supplies 174 factor contrasts and
18 additional joint-policy contrasts, each evaluated on eight metrics.
All six capacities and all contrast components remain in the evidence package.
Comparisons away from 10\% also measure transfer from the fixed training capacity.

\paragraph{Post-hoc simultaneous sensitivity.}
We supplement the original unadjusted intervals with 20,000 centered crossed
bootstrap draws~\cite{pigeonhole}. The family maximum of absolute standardized
errors supplies one common critical value; scales are the conditional standard
deviations of the crossed bootstrap, not re-estimated inside each draw.
Pairing across methods and metrics is retained. We report the 36 inflation
feedback contrasts, the same contrasts on three co-primary outcomes (108),
and all 1,536 primary contrast/metric combinations. A source-wise Bonferroni
sensitivity avoids requiring cross-source independence. Resampling maxima
are established multiple-testing tools~\cite{romanowolf}; this is a single-step
sensitivity analysis, not a transfer of their finite-sample guarantee.

The adverse feedback count falls from 34 unadjusted intervals to 31, 27 and
20 under these three families (Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays}). Source-wise bands
retain 31, 27 and 19. The original 36 adverse point estimates remain unchanged.
No family certifies that every contrast is harmful. With only ten seeds and
ten batches, approximate simultaneous bands do not establish finite-sample
coverage, fresh-cohort confirmation or training-population uncertainty.



\paragraph{Feedback does not explain a beneficial effect in this design.}
Define a feedback contrast as no-policy-feedback loss minus policy-feedback
loss, holding the initial objective, score and allocator fixed. For positive
expected log-quote inflation, all 36 point estimates at 10\% were negative;
34 unadjusted intervals excluded zero in the unfavorable direction.
The two intervals crossing zero corresponded to MUCars with the mask initial
objective and suppression acquisition. No feedback contrast passed the point
gate of at least 10\% inflation reduction with at most 5\% honest-MAE increase.
This is a negative result for the implemented two-round, epoch-refreshed,
weak-response-search training procedure. The strong-search means in
Section~\ref{sec:completion_mechanisms} narrow its interpretation; they do not
alter the original counts.

Table~\ref{tab:factorial_counts} summarizes all primary directions.
\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{lrrrr}
\toprule
Source & Feedback harms & Harm intervals & Robust-init gains & Joint gates\\
\midrule
Synthetic & 12/12 & 12/12 & 4/12 & 0/6\\
JUCars & 12/12 & 12/12 & 12/12 & 0/6\\
MUCars & 12/12 & 10/12 & 12/12 & 0/6\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Original weak-search implementation: primary-capacity factorial diagnostics. These counts do not apply to strong-search retraining (Section~\ref{sec:completion_mechanisms}). Counts refer to positive
expected log-quote inflation. Feedback harms and robust-initialization gains
are point-estimate directions. Harm intervals are 95\% unadjusted intervals
excluding zero adversely for the capped primary evaluation. The uncapped
MUCars sensitivity also excludes zero in 10/12 intervals
(Section~\ref{sec:full_validation}). A joint gate compares no feedback/top-$B$ with
policy feedback/minimax under a fixed initial objective and score, requiring
at least 10\% inflation reduction and at most 5\% honest-MAE increase.
Counts are descriptive across correlated contrasts, not independent trials.}
\label{tab:factorial_counts}
\end{table}

Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} displays the suppression-acquisition comparisons.



\paragraph{Initial-objective effects depend on the source.}
The robust initial objective lowered mean inflation in all twelve MUCars
conditional contrasts. Reductions ranged from 12.43\% to 33.40\%, all twelve
unadjusted intervals excluded zero favorably, and all twelve passed the point
gate. Honest MAE also decreased in these comparisons. On JUCars, twelve point
estimates favored the robust objective but only two intervals excluded zero
favorably. On synthetic data, only four point estimates favored it, and six
intervals excluded zero in the adverse direction. Thus the controlled
initial-objective factor has a stronger source-dependent signature than a
beneficial feedback effect. These are newly trained matched models, so they
do not retrospectively identify the cause of the original SAV/static-robust
comparison.

Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} shows the no-feedback suppression comparisons.



\paragraph{A positive interaction can mean less harm.}
For synthetic data with the mask objective and suppression acquisition, the
feedback--allocation interaction was $D=0.004917$, with an unadjusted interval
$[0.002378,0.007586]$. Feedback increased inflation under both allocators:
from 0.010871 to 0.018969 under top-$B$, and from 0.010638 to 0.013818 under
minimax. The positive interaction measures the smaller increase under minimax;
it does not show that minimax feedback improved on its no-feedback control.
Across all sources, none of eighteen joint changes from no feedback/top-$B$
to policy feedback/minimax passed the point gate. The three favorable MUCars
joint point estimates were small and their intervals crossed zero. Inflation,
honest error, attacked error and failed-query effects must therefore be read
jointly rather than inferred from the interaction sign.

\paragraph{Mechanism and practical boundary.}
The factorial experiment isolates implemented training and allocation choices,
but does not identify a unique cause of its feedback damage.
Section~\ref{sec:completion_mechanisms} intervenes on response-search strength
and score-refresh frequency; stronger search removes some damage, while
capacity transfer is not separately identified. These computationally checked
development interventions qualify the negative result without establishing a sole cause.
The appropriate practical comparison is the matched no-feedback control with
the same initial objective, score and capacity. Choosing feedback from a
positive interaction alone is unsupported. A risk score is also not a universal
fallback: its feedback-trained cells are among the adverse cases. Performance
on a new cohort requires renewed multiobjective validation; point Pareto flags
in the present cohort are descriptive, not probabilistic dominance guarantees.


\paragraph{Absolute outcomes qualify an inflation-only ranking.}
Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} holds the robust initial objective and suppression
acquisition fixed. Minimax feedback increased JUCars inflation from 0.010432
to 0.011855 while reducing attacked MAE from 6902.07 to 6899.03 and lowering
query failures. On MUCars, the same feedback contrast increased both inflation
and attacked error. Thus improving prediction error need not reduce the
seller's gain over the model's own honest quote. Of the 432 source/capacity/cell
settings after aggregating seeds, 37 contained a nonconverged batch; eight of
these were synthetic primary-capacity settings. The maximum recorded remaining
regret was 0.177513 log units. All were retained.





\subsection{Strong controls and the original candidate comparison}
The original SAV study supplies a comparator check rather than a factorial
attribution. Its ten-seed extension covers 30 source--seed pairs, 1,860 cells
and 1.86 million repeated outcomes. At 10\% capacity, SAV inflation was
0.021175 on synthetic data, 0.014988 on JUCars and 0.007340 on MUCars; the
corresponding static-robust values were 0.012084, 0.012082 and 0.006511.
The static-robust-minus-SAV crossed intervals were
$[-0.012355,-0.006323]$, $[-0.007025,0.000777]$ and $[-0.001870,0.000210]$,
respectively. These comparisons do not support consistent SAV superiority;
intervals including zero also do not establish equivalence. Appendix~\ref{app:original}
retains all five original comparators for each source, including strong
benefit-head controls and weaker masking/no-gap variants. These original models
must not be substituted for the matched factorial objectives.

Competitive controls materially change the original-method story. In the
audited three-seed ordinary-benefit extension, reference-minus-SAV inflation
differences are $-0.00998$ on synthetic and $-0.00432$ on JUCars; their conditional
intervals are $[-0.01260,-0.00791]$ and $[-0.00598,-0.00285]$. The ACO-1 control
also beats SAV on these sources, with SAV-minus-ACO differences 0.00995 and
0.00431 and intervals $[0.00725,0.01345]$ and $[0.00291,0.00588]$.
These are separate original-protocol three-seed comparisons, not new
ten-seed matched-factorial effects. A universal SAV advantage is not reproduced
against these strong controls. MUCars remains conditional: ordinary benefit
favors SAV by 0.00089, while the ACO comparison spans zero; the largest-cap
equal-tuning experiment favors SAV on MUCars but reverses on the other sources.
Appendix~\ref{app:comparators} preserves all fitting, tuning and adaptation
boundaries. The main implication is to compare strategy-aware valuation with
competitive predictive acquisition before attributing a gain to strategic
feedback.



\subsection{Allocation changes on frozen models}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} Score-minimax allocation can help with a robust
price model, but the same intervention can worsen inflation with a masking
model. It is not a universal replacement for top-$B$.

This separate experiment retains the original three seeds and frozen price
models. It crosses three sources, two price models, three public acquisition
scores and two allocators at 10\% capacity: 108 cells and 108,000 outcome rows.
Seller responses are recomputed for each allocator. The separate evaluator
reconstructs 1,080 method/batch cells, 1,134,324 legal unilateral deviations,
model outputs, allocations and expected-error arithmetic. A second checker
rebuilds all eighteen contrasts, five metrics, resampling intervals, decision
gates and pointwise dominance flags from the raw records. This is an allocation
intervention conditional on frozen models, not the full feedback-training study.

Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} retains all eighteen contrasts. Only robust-benefit
and robust-suppression on MUCars meet the inherited heuristic point criteria of at
least 10\% inflation reduction and at most 5\% honest-MAE increase. They reduce
inflation by 13.70\% and 13.05\%, but increase failed-query probability by
0.060 and 0.128 percentage points. Honest MAE is unchanged in every contrast
under these replay semantics. Ten of eighteen contrasts increase inflation;
four have a positive lower endpoint for the inflation reduction. JUCars
robust-benefit improves by 9.985\%, which does not meet the 10\% threshold.
No threshold is relaxed after observing this near miss.

\small

\normalsize

The multiobjective results qualify the apparent gains. MUCars robust-benefit
and robust-suppression also reduce expected attacked MAE by 7.17 and 7.67
source-currency units, but their failed-query increases prevent Pareto
dominance. JUCars robust-benefit and robust-suppression increase attacked MAE
by 2.06 and 0.62 units despite reducing inflation. Only JUCars robust-risk and
synthetic robust-suppression weakly dominate top-$B$ on all four point metrics;
neither has an inflation interval excluding zero. These point comparisons use
a numerical tie tolerance, not a claim of practical equivalence.

Ten top-$B$ response batches fail the convergence criterion (six synthetic
masking-benefit and four synthetic masking-suppression); their maximum
unilateral regret is 0.095752. The minimax responses have zero recorded final
regret over the enumerated legal unilateral actions. All batches are retained.
Thus the intervention includes its response-search consequences, and the
observed reversals cannot be attributed solely to equilibrium allocation quality.
The score objective remains a surrogate and does not prove general incentive
optimality. The completed factorial study evaluates whether training feedback changes these
conditional effects.



\subsection{Continuous effects and decision-threshold sensitivity}
The inherited 10\% inflation-reduction/5\% honest-MAE gate is a heuristic,
not an externally validated minimum important difference or a preregistration
claim. We retain it to avoid retrospective threshold selection, report the
continuous effects, and vary inflation cutoffs over 0, 5, 10, 15 and 20\%
and honest-error allowances over 0, 1, 5 and 10\%.

At a 5\% honest-error allowance, the frozen allocation study has 8/18, 4/18,
2/18, 0/18 and 0/18 passing contrasts across the respective inflation cutoffs. Its JUCars
robust-benefit reduction is approximately 9.985\%, so classifying it below a
10\% gate is not a substantive discontinuity in usefulness. In the matched
joint-policy family, the zero-cutoff gate retains 3/18 contrasts, all small
MUCars gains; 0/18 pass any inflation cutoff of 5\% or more. Full grids and
unrounded effects are supplied. A deployment threshold requires a platform's
error/query-cost preferences, which this retrospective study has not elicited.



\subsection{Positive costs change the conditional feedback ranking}
We recompute responses under per-hidden-field costs 0.005 and 0.02 in log-quote
utility units, and heterogeneous costs $0.02h_i$, where a fixed listing hash
gives $h_i\in[0.5,1.5)$. These are stipulated post-hoc sensitivities, not measured
human effort or monetary sanctions. All 108 positive-cost cells retain the
robust initial objective and suppression score, three original fitting seeds,
both feedback settings and both allocators. Four response starts and twenty
sweeps are unchanged. Costs enter report choice; they are not simply subtracted
from saved zero-cost outcomes. Independent algebraic replay and every legal
unilateral utility corroborate the returned profiles.

Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} shows a ranking reversal on synthetic top-$B$.
Uniform 0.02 and heterogeneous costs have lower mean inflation with feedback
than without it; the heterogeneous direction is positive for all three seeds,
while the uniform 0.02 direction is positive for only one. At heterogeneous
cost, feedback also lowers honest MAE by 4.293 synthetic price units, attacked
MAE by 12.198 units and failed queries by 0.100 percentage points. This is
descriptive mean Pareto improvement within that declared subset, not an
uncertainty-qualified or universal dominance result. Failure remains high
in absolute terms (9.4\% without and 9.3\% with feedback per listing).



Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} separates concealment deterrence from the paired feedback effects.



The reversal accompanies strong concealment deterrence. At uniform cost
0.02, synthetic top-$B$ mean hidden-field counts are bounded by
$[0.130,0.165]$ without feedback and $[0.130,0.181]$ with it, versus directly
observed zero-cost means 0.996 and 0.957. These bounds retain nonconverged
profiles rather than treating the inflation--net-deviation gap as exact cost.
The legal net-deviation envelope falls by 77.5\% and 81.1\%, respectively.
Failed queries reach 9.47\% and 9.40\% per listing; at 10\% capacity, at least
94.7\% and 94.0\% of actual queries fail. Thus the local ranking reversal
coexists with suppressed concealment and predominantly unsuccessful
verification; it does not isolate improved feedback quality as its cause.
Constructively, deterrence moves this subset toward ordinary valuation:
at both 0.02 cost specifications, feedback no longer worsens mean inflation
and lowers mean honest error, attacked error and query failure. This is a
conditional descriptive benefit, not a fully nonstrategic limit: residual
concealment remains positive. Appendix~\ref{app:mechanism_diagnostics} gives
the utility-based bounds.

JUCars and MUCars retain adverse mean inflation directions for both allocators
under every declared cost regime in this subset. Twenty positive-cost
seed/cell batches remain nonconverged and are retained. Therefore the original
36/36 zero-cost direction describes its evaluated regime, not a cost-invariant
property of feedback training. The full three-source cost table is in the
evidence package; no winning cost distribution is substituted for the zero-cost
primary experiment.



\subsection{Full-validation evaluation coverage}\label{app:fullvalidation_details}
An additional frozen-model evaluation removes both real-source caps: all
1,374 JUCars and 10,672 MUCars validation records form fourteen and 107 batches,
respectively, using the same ten fitting seeds. Final batches contain 74 and
72 records, each with capacity seven. All 480 cells, 29,040 seed/cell batches
and 2,891,040 repeated outcomes pass algebraic allocation, bit-transition,
metric and complete unilateral-utility checks, covering 31,269,840 legal
deviations. All returned real-source batches meet the finite unilateral-regret
tolerance; this does not certify arbitrary actions or globally optimal joint
responses. The first ten batches reuse the original prediction/outcome bytes
through symlinks, with original receipt provenance retained in the extended
bindings; only the remaining batches are newly evaluated. No model is retrained.

All 24 real-source feedback inflation point estimates remain adverse.
Ten-thousand-draw exploratory intervals exclude zero adversely for all twelve
JUCars comparisons and ten MUCars comparisons; both MUCars masking-objective,
suppression-score comparisons span zero. These unadjusted intervals weight
batches by record counts, including the partial batches. They do not add
independent training records or make either retrospective source confirmatory.
The original capped factorial remains the declared primary comparison.

An exact conditional seed/batch variance decomposition, including weighted
partial batches, attributes 98.39\% and 98.28\% to fitting-seed main effects
for the uncapped MUCars masking/suppression top-$B$ and minimax contrasts.
Their capped shares are 90.97\% and 87.42\%; these compare different cohort
and weighting designs, not a pure replication-size change. Interval-width
ratios alone do not identify the components. Under the empirical conditional design,
thirty instead of ten independent fitting seeds would project standard
deviations to approximately 58\% of their current values, while eliminating
only batch resampling noise leaves approximately 99\%. These are design
projections, not newly trained results. The stable small estimates and
crossing-zero intervals establish neither zero effect nor equivalence;
fitting randomness on fixed training records also does not quantify
training-population uncertainty. Details are in
Appendix~\ref{app:mechanism_diagnostics}.


Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} shows absolute components alongside their shares.



The accompanying \path{audit-protocol/} artifact provides a machine-readable
worked contract, an execution protocol and a standard-library validator of
six directly bound evidence files. Six invalid-contract controls are rejected.
It is an explicit post-hoc protocol, not preregistration or a certificate of
scientific validity or independent end-to-end reproduction.




\subsection{Recomputed cap responses and multiobjective trade-offs}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} The cap's inflation reductions survive the saved
response heuristic in this study, but attacked error and failed-query rates
can worsen. This is an empirical result, not an extension of the fixed-peer theorem.

We recomputed reports for every source/seed/estimator/capacity/rule cell, using
the original four starts and up to 20 sweeps, frozen public scores and unchanged
mixture weights. The uncapped baseline uses the separately corroborated original
responses. All 432 new cells and 432,000 listing outcomes were replayed by an
separate full-sort auditor. It checked 5,300 supported complete-rule batch
allocations and 5,570,112 legal rule--deviation outcomes. The auditor reconstructs
final quotations, currency absolute errors, query failures and remaining unilateral
regret; it does not certify a globally optimal joint response or every search step.

Appendix~\ref{app:historical_displays} reports the primary robust-estimator comparison.


Figure~\ref{fig:cap_tradeoffs} displays the two principal trade-off coordinates.



The consequential finding is the joint cost: attacked MAE increases in 94
of 432 recomputed-response cells and failed-query probability increases in
145, while honest MAE stays unchanged. Inflation falls in 279 cells and ties
in 153; attacked MAE falls in 185 and ties in 153, and query failures fall
in 4 and tie in 283. All counts remain descriptive. The fixed-peer theorem
already establishes pointwise cap monotonicity and is not an empirical
effectiveness test. Here peers' responses are recomputed, so cross-profile
monotonicity is not supplied by that theorem either. The useful question is
whether any inflation reduction survives the attacked-error and query-failure
trade-offs, not how many cells exhibit the cap's intended quote reduction.

Thirty-three capped cells contain at least one batch with remaining unilateral
regret above the convergence tolerance, compared with 34 original cells. The
maximum capped regret is 0.204423 log units. We retain these cases. In the primary
robust benefit comparison, one synthetic seed has one nonconverged batch, with
maximum regret 0.029232; the JUCars and MUCars primary benefit batches pass the
specified local regret check. Therefore no result is described as a certified
market equilibrium.

For the 12 primary source/rule contrasts, we compute unadjusted 95\% percentile
intervals from 1,000 paired resamples of the ten shared batches, first averaging
the three fixed fitted-seed outcomes within each batch. These are conditional
evaluation-batch intervals, not training-population intervals or 30 independent
batch replicates. For benefit, the absolute inflation-reduction intervals are
$[0.000388,0.001252]$ (synthetic), $[0.001207,0.001586]$ (JUCars), and
$[0.000842,0.000995]$ (MUCars). The JUCars and MUCars point reductions satisfy
the original 10\% inflation/5\% honest-error criterion with a positive conditional
interval, but that criterion does not constrain attacked error or query failures.
In particular, JUCars attacked MAE increases by 0.080\%.

Risk had no change in the earlier primary fixed-profile table, but can change
under the newly computed profiles. The comparisons condition on different peer
reports, so these findings are compatible. The existing fixed-profile theorem
must not be used as a common-response or Pareto-dominance guarantee.
